Combining them as independent gives the four-in-five range above. They are not fully independent on the downside: a risk-off episode strengthens the franc and weakens private-pay demand together, and a lost launch window and channel losses arrive together (the correlation the first set identified). Morgan Stanley's own cases for the same year show the same shape: bull CHF 13.89, base CHF 12.86, bear CHF 10.49, that is +8% and -18%.

The shape through time. The cone does not widen smoothly. It is narrow until 18 May 2027, widens at that event (the FY2028 guide and the franc assumption stated at spot), and is widest at the November 2027 print, for the reasons in D-03.

Evidence on how wrong 18-month forecasts have been. Half-year core profit has missed or beaten consensus by 5% to 10% at four of the last five half-year prints (November 2024 -5%, November 2025 -5%, May 2026 +5%, November 2026 +10%). Consensus EPS for each of FY2026, FY2027 and FY2028 has ranged about 25% to 35% from low to high over its life, and today stands 12% to 19% below its peak for each year (FactSet), mostly through the franc, the tax rate and the consumer exit. Two of those three causes are spent. Against that, Sonova's organic growth has the lowest volatility in the peer group, which is why the operating part of the cone (about plus or minus 4%) is narrow for a medical-technology company.

Disagreement with the second set. Its range for FY2028 (-8% to +5%) was right in shape; the centre is about 2% lower and consensus is no longer at the centre.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

D469th of 12

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: of limited help. Sonova itself will say nothing scheduled on trading between now and 18 May 2027, so for six months the case is monitored almost entirely through other parties' data. That data is unusually good for one driver (the VA channel, monthly) and for the currency (daily, with published sensitivities), and absent for the drivers that carry most of the profit: independent audiologists, the second-half margin and the retail business. What the company discloses at its two yearly events is clear and well structured, but it withholds the two splits that would let an outsider follow the thesis (volume against price, wholesale against retail profit), and it has changed its headline measure once and will change its presentation again in April 2027.

The calendar to 18 May 2027 (who speaks, and on what).

  • Monthly, about four weeks after each month: VA purchasing data, October to March. The first full months of EON, including the smaller Sphere model timed for the VA's November window.
  • Done, 24-Nov-26: Demant's Capital Markets Day cut its mid-term organic growth target to 5% to 7% (from 6% to 8%) on a heavier retail mix, kept a market view of 4% to 6% with flat prices, and confirmed a buyback of at least DKK 3bn by end-2027.
  • Around the turn of the year: the Amplifon and GN Hearing closing; the FDA decision on Integro (the company has said it may slip past calendar year-end); Swiss National Bank decisions in December and March (Swiss inflation rose to 1.5% in October).
  • February to March 2027: Demant's full-year results and 2027 guidance; GN's Capital Markets Day; Cochlear's half-year; Amplifon's full-year results and, in the first half of 2027, its post-deal Capital Markets Day (the best source on how fast it leaves Sonova).
  • By 31 March 2027: completion of the Consumer Hearing sale (proceeds undisclosed).
  • At any time: a Section 232 proclamation; an acquisition announcement.
  • 18 May 2027: FY2027 results and the FY2028 guide, which is when the company next gives the franc assumption, the tax rate after the safe harbour, the first report on the savings programme, inventory days and any capital-allocation statement.

Driver by driver.

Two practical points for the memo Analyst.

  1. State the VA thresholds on value share. The first set's alarms (total VA share below 50% for a month; below 48% for two months) were set against a value share of 53.5% (September 2026, Jefferies). The unit share for the same month is 50.9% (Bernstein, Morgan Stanley), and broker headlines often quote units. On units Sonova is already within a point of the "alarm"; on value it is 3.5 points above it. The custom-device threshold (40%) compares with 42% to 43% in September.
  2. The measure itself is hard to follow. Two successive analyst sets working from the company's and brokers' material put the FY2026 core EBIT base 2% too high, enough to move the forecast from "top of guidance" to "three points above it". FactSet shows two different adjusted EBIT figures for the same year (CHF 743m on its annual line, CHF 761m from its two half-years). From 1 April 2027 the new presentation standard (IFRS 18) moves currency gains and losses into operating profit, with restated comparatives arriving only with the first-half FY2028 report (AlphaSense currency report, citing the interim report). The print that most tests the thesis will therefore come on a changed basis for the second time in 18 months.

What is helpful. Guidance is given in stated blocks with the market assumption, the currency effect at spot and the excluded costs quantified; the second-half investment step-up was flagged in advance; segment sales are split into organic and acquired; working-capital days are disclosed. Demant's quarterly reporting and the monthly VA series make this an easier industry to follow between prints than most of medical technology.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

C564th of 12

Layer 3 · Risks

The Investment Analyst's heading How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into operational drivers, onto revenue, cost and margin lines, and through to EPS; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to EPS?

Judgement: the internal chain is clear from the company's statements down to group core EBIT, and breaks in three places: between wholesale and retail profit, between volume and price, and between core EBIT and EPS, where the financial result and the tax rate have recently moved EPS as much as operations have. The external chain is unusually easy to quantify for four items (the franc, the VA channel, a tariff, Amplifon) and opaque for the channel where most of the profit is made (independents) and for retail customer acquisition costs.

The internal chain, link by link.

  • Sales. "Market 3% to 5%, plus share gains of 1 to 3 points, plus bolt-ons of 1 to 2 points" maps directly onto reported lines: segment sales in local currency, with retail split into organic and acquired (5.2% and 1.3 points in the first half). Clear.
  • Gross margin. The operations programme (CHF 50m of cost, CHF 90m of savings by the fourth year, net savings from the second) is tied to a stated 1.5 points of gross margin by 2030 (March 2026 strategy update) and shows in the core gross margin line (74.7% in the first half); the cost shows in non-core items (CHF 35m to 40m guided for FY2027). Clear, though the company gives no yearly phasing.
  • Operating costs. "Plus 1 to 2 points of operating leverage, minus 1 to 3 points of investment in R&D, IT and digital" maps onto the functional cost lines (R&D 5.9% of sales; more than 1% of sales for digital and IT). The range of the investment line (one to three points) is as wide as the whole margin gain it is set against, so the statement does not pin down the net. This is where the previous programme's savings disappeared.
  • Core EBIT. Guided as growth in constant currency, with the currency effect given separately. Clear, apart from the base (D-05).
  • Segment profit. Only Hearing Instruments and Cochlear Implants are reported. Wholesale (above 30% margin) and retail (mid-teens) are not separated, so the largest mix effect in the model, the dilution from buying clinics, cannot be traced from disclosure.
  • Core EBIT to EPS. No guidance on the financial result, no EPS guidance, a tax rate given only as the expected rate for the year. In the first half of FY2027 core EPS rose CHF 1.41 (CHF 3.96 to 5.37, +35.6%) on core EBIT growth of 21.4%: about CHF 0.94 came from operating profit and about CHF 0.41 from the financial result (CHF -31.0m a year earlier, CHF -1.7m now). Nearly 30% of the EPS growth that the market applauded came from below the operating line and will not repeat. The reverse is in my FY2028: core EBIT +7%, core EPS +6%, with the first half at about +2%.

The external chain.

  • Franc to EPS: clear and mechanical. Translation moves sales one for one and core EBIT about 1.6 times; the company's sensitivities allow the effect to be computed on any day. A 5% move in all major currencies is about 6.5% of core operating profit.
  • VA to wholesale: clear. Monthly value, Sonova's share, prices fixed by the May 2025 contract (a 26% price increase for Sonova) until about 2030.
  • Tariff to gross margin: clear in arithmetic, unclear in probability and timing. About CHF 16m per 10 points of duty (Morgan Stanley); 160 basis points of gross margin at 25% before mitigation (Rothschild & Co Redburn); no pass-through in the VA; six to twelve months to revalidate a production move.
  • Amplifon to sales: clear in size (CHF 50m to 60m at EUR 150 to 200 a unit), dependent on Amplifon for timing.
  • Payers and channel mix to margin: unclear. Revenue per unit can fall while profit rises (Costco at a high-20s margin) or fall with profit (managed care). Without channel margins, external price data cannot be turned into a margin forecast.
  • Independents to wholesale profit: opaque. Channel checks point both ways (complaints about Phonak's price rigidity, service and the Costco brand; Phonak at 55% of fittings in one clinic and requested by name; a Barclays survey pointing to Phonak gaining two points of share in six months). This is the channel with the highest prices (USD 600 to 1,100 a unit) and no data.

The consequence for predictability. An outsider can forecast Sonova's group sales and core EBIT in local currency from stated blocks with fair confidence, and can translate them into francs on any given exchange rate. What cannot be traced are the mix of profit between wholesale and retail and the line from EBIT to EPS in any one half-year. The practical rule: judge each print on core EBIT in constant currency and on wholesale growth, not on core EPS growth.

What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

D3620th of 38

Layer 1 · Risks

The Investment Analyst's heading What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector or market?

Judgement: sentiment risk is high because the shares are priced above every broker target median, at the 80th percentile of their two-year multiple, after a 47% twelve-month rise, in a cohort the sector notes call "fully known and in the price"; several monthly or event-driven triggers could start a de-rating regardless of Sonova's fundamentals.

Triggers.

  • Monthly VA data (published about a month after each month): a public, monthly share series that the market reads as a scorecard of the launch window; one weak month for EON or a jump for Zeal or Reveal could move the stock 5% to 10%.
  • Demant's Capital Markets Day (24 November 2026): an expected cut of Demant's mid-term organic target to 5% to 7% could be read as a cap on industry growth; Jefferies says Demant's +75% rally is complete.
  • Peer events: an Amplifon-GN integration stumble, independents' boycott headlines, a WS Audiology launch success, or Cochlear Ltd guidance.
  • Sector and factor rotation: healthcare became "the home for semiconductor and technology money" (Jefferies, October 2026), which can leave; European medtech consumables sit at the 14th percentile of their own multiple range while Sonova sits at about the 60th, so a rotation to cheap medtech or back to technology would hit Sonova first.
  • Franc headlines: European sovereign stress (French risk) lifting the franc.
  • Positioning: short interest spiked above 19% of free float in late 2025; FactSet buy share is only 17% and fell 10 points in a month; price 290.6 against median target CHF 270 and mean CHF 276; the dashboard's TSR/Valuation rating is F after the memo's modal return was re-based.
  • "AI winners" de-rating: Sonova's narrative leans on AI chips; a broad de-rating of AI-linked stories could reach it.

C-70

Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

C+5812th of 38

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: the new chief executive's behaviour so far is "guide low, raise once with H1 evidence, leave room": a single, sizeable mid-year raise with the year's arithmetic still conservative. Before him, the company's pattern was the reverse (guide to a range, deliver the low end, occasionally warn).

Bernard era (two data points). May 2026: FY2027 guidance (sales 5% to 8%, core EBIT 7% to 10%, market 2% to 4%, FX drag) set below where H2 FY2026 run-rates pointed; consensus at the time already modelled a better market. November 2026: raised sales floor by 1 point and core EBIT by 3 points, while leaving H2 needing only 1% to 6% EBIT growth; flagged an H2 investment step-up in advance. Morgan Stanley: "Guidance raise still looks conservative"; brokers speculate another upgrade could follow. This resembles the Wisdom Library's "sandbagging precision management" archetype in its early stage; two observations are not a record.

Pre-Bernard pattern. August 2022 profit warning (EBITA growth 12% to 18% cut to 6% to 10%); low-end delivery in FY2022/23 and FY2025/26; withdrawal of mid-term guidance in late 2025; the March 2026 reset widened the mid-term range.

Peer comparison. Demant: four downward revisions in two years, then two raises in 2026. Amplifon: cautious guide, modest misses. GN: three years of over-promising.

For the model: expect FY2027 to land at or slightly above the top of the raised ranges (sales about 8%, core EBIT about 13%) and the FY2028 guide in May 2027 to be set conservatively again (for example sales 5% to 7% local currency and core EBIT high single to low double digits), with the franc assumption at the then-spot rate.

C-69

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

D5221st of 38

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: last year, beating; last three years, mixed (meets in local currency, misses in francs); last five years, below average, with a 2022 warning, a 2023 to 2024 contract loss and low-end deliveries. In a soft market with franc strength (comparable to 2023 to 2025) Sonova met local-currency targets but its reported numbers disappointed.

One year. FY2026 (May 2026): full-year sales +5.9% local currency against +5.5% consensus; H2 normalised EBITA CHF 480m against CHF 456m (+5%); margin 25.4% against 24.1%; normalised EPS CHF 10.42 against CHF 9.85; cochlear implants missed by 14%. H1 FY2027 (November 2026): sales +1% against consensus; wholesale +11% against 9.5%; core EBIT CHF 384m against CHF 348m (+10%); core EPS CHF 5.37 against CHF 4.52 (+19%); implants slightly below.

Three years. FY2024: revenue -3.0% in francs, normalised EBITA -8.2% (loss of a large US contract); FY2025: recovery, sales +7.6% local currency, adjusted EBITA +7.4% local currency (one source; another series shows different year labels); H1 FY2026: normalised EBITA in line with guidance but 5% below absolute consensus; FY2026: low end of sales guidance, earnings beat.

Five years. August 2022: EBITA growth guidance cut from 12% to 18% to 6% to 10% (slower US private market, shipping costs); FY2022/23 low end; late 2025 mid-term guidance withdrawn; March 2026 floor cut. Short sellers ran campaigns into successive 2025 guidance downgrades (Bernstein).

Comparable conditions. Strong market plus new platform (2018 Marvel, 2022 Lumity, 2024 Infinio): Sonova outgrew the market; soft market plus strong franc (2023 to 2025): reported earnings stalled. The current setup (market recovering, new platform, FX neutral) is the favourable configuration.

P-06

After assessing the track record of delivery in greater detail, how much weight should the forecasts for the next 18 months to four years carry, and in which direction should they be leaned?

C5510th of 26

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: weight by year, not as a block. FY2027 deserves high weight (H1 is reported, guidance is conservative, a new team has an incentive to beat): lean to the top of guidance on core EBIT (+13%) and the upper half on sales (about 7.4% in local currency). FY2028 deserves medium weight: lean neutral on EPS, up on margin, down on reported revenue (the franc). FY2029 to FY2031 deserve low-to-medium weight: lean below consensus on revenue and EPS, above it on margin, because the five-year record says Sonova delivers its local-currency plan and misses in francs.

The record, separated into its two halves.

  • Local-currency delivery (good): the lowest organic-growth volatility in the peer group (standard deviation 3.0 against Demant's 4.5); wholesale share gains for eleven quarters; FY2026 sales +5.9% against +5.5% consensus, H2 normalised EBITA CHF 480m against CHF 456m; H1 FY2027 core EBIT CHF 384m against CHF 348m (+10%), core EPS CHF 5.37 against CHF 4.52 (+19%). Cochlear Implants is the persistent miss (-14% against consensus in FY2026, slightly below in H1 FY2027).
  • Franc delivery and guidance behaviour before 2025 (poor): an August 2022 cut to EBITA growth guidance (12% to 18% to 6% to 10%); low-end delivery in FY2023 and FY2026; the FY2024 Costco loss; mid-term guidance withdrawn in late 2025 and its floor cut in March 2026; normalised EPS in francs flat for four years (CHF 11.14, 10.06, 10.43, 10.42).
  • The new team (two data points): a conservative first guide (May 2026: sales 5% to 8%, core EBIT 7% to 10%, market 2% to 4%, franc drag 2 to 3 points), then a single sizeable raise (November 2026) that still leaves H2 needing only 1% to 6% core EBIT growth. Two observations are a pattern of intent, not a record.

Confidence bands I would attach (my judgement, before market or tail events).

  • FY2027 core EPS CHF 12.07: 80% band CHF 11.8 to 12.35 (±2% to 3%). Upside: H2 wholesale above 8%, lower financial costs; downside: Integro delay, a weak VA run for EON, a franc spike in Q1 calendar 2027 (hedges cover 1 to 6 months).
  • FY2028 core EPS CHF 12.77: 80% band CHF 11.7 to 13.4 (-8% / +5%). The skew is negative because the largest single swing factor, the franc, is asymmetric in a risk-off regime, and because the launch window can be lost faster than it can be won (Zeal took 13 points of VA custom share in a quarter).
  • FY2029 to FY2031: bands of ±12% to 15%; the next platform (about August 2028), the deployment of CHF 0.5bn to 1bn of balance sheet, and five years of franc drift dominate.

Direction of lean, by line.

  • Revenue in francs: below consensus from FY2028 (my 4,074 against 4,176; 4,466 against 4,732 in FY2030). Reasons: consensus carries no franc drag (it grows at local-currency rates from an FY2026 base), and local-currency growth of 7.4% (FY2028) and 6.5% (FY2029) in consensus sits at the top of the market-plus-share-plus-bolt-on arithmetic once Amplifon and share fade are included.
  • Margin: above consensus (22.8% against 22.1% in FY2027; 23.9% against 23.8% in FY2030 in core EBIT). Consensus still contains stale pre-results estimates and does not fully credit the gross-margin reset.
  • EPS: in line for FY2027 to FY2028, about 3% below for FY2029 to FY2030 with buybacks, about 5% to 6% below without.
  • Free cash flow: in line for FY2027 to FY2028 on the consensus definition (before lease payments: mine CHF 705m and 777m against 684m and 773m), 4% to 5% below for FY2029 to FY2030.

What would change the weights. Upward: an FY2028 guide in May 2027 of sales at or above 6% local currency and core EBIT high single digits or better with a stated franc assumption near neutral; a buyback announcement; VA total share holding above 52% through March 2027. Downward: VA total share below 50% for a month (the first set's alarm), wholesale H2 FY2027 growth below 5%, EUR/CHF below 0.90, a Section 232 decision without the Nairobi exemption.

Sonova's financial forecasts show the first sustained growth of franc earnings in five years (core EPS about +17% in FY2027, then about 6% to 8% a year, a CAGR of about 9% to FY2031) on local-currency operations that change little (growth about 6% a year, margins up about 35 basis points a year after a one-time FY2027 reset). FY2027 is high-confidence and sits at the top of guidance on core EBIT. FY2028 consensus EPS is fair, but it is reached by a different route: lower revenue (franc), higher margin and capital deployment. FY2029 to FY2030 consensus requires either a neutral franc or deployment of the balance sheet, and my base case gets within 3% only by assuming both a modest franc drag and buybacks. The forecasts are concentrated in a few drivers: the franc (about 40% of the difference from history), the outcome of the crowded launch window (share gains of 1.5 points in FY2028 fading to 0.5), the net-of-reinvestment savings (about half of CHF 90m), and the decision on a balance sheet that falls to about 0.7x leverage by March 2027. Operating and financial leverage are not risks; the franc and the window are. For the third set: the next 18 months are predictable on operations (H1 reported, guidance conservative, cash strong) and less predictable on reported francs and on the H1 FY2028 deceleration; for the fourth set: consensus has the margin roughly right, the revenue too high in francs, and no capital deployment.

P-08

After assessing below-operating-profit cost items (e.g. tax, interest, one-offs, minority investments, etc.) and share buybacks or issuance in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share, net income and free cash flow over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

C-5612th of 26

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: over FY2020 to FY2026 the items below operating profit were a large hidden headwind: the tax rate rose from 2.6% to 8.9% (FY2020 to FY2024) to 16% to 18% (FY2025 to FY2026), costing about 9% of EPS between FY2023 and FY2026 on its own, while the share count stopped falling. Over FY2027 to FY2031 they turn roughly neutral to slightly positive: the tax step-up is largely complete (a drift from 17% to 18% costs about 1% of EPS), interest is negligible, and a buyback restart adds 2% to 3% by FY2030 to FY2031. The one trap is FY2027 itself: unusually low financial costs flatter FY2027 EPS by about CHF 0.15 and depress FY2028 growth by about 1.3 points when they normalise. Free cash flow roughly doubles from the FactSet history because working capital, capex and payables normalise and EBITDA grows.

Tax. Reported tax rate (FactSet): 2.6%, 2.5%, 8.9%, 8.1%, 5.9% (FY2020 to FY2024), 17.6% (FY2025), 16.0% (FY2026). The cause was structural: the Zurich cantonal reform ended the privileged holding regime (2019 to 2024 transition) and then the Swiss implementation of the OECD 15% minimum tax (Pillar Two). The company expects about 16.9% for FY2027, including one-time effects. Peers: Straumann rose from 17.9% to 21.3% (2024 to 2025) under Pillar Two; Demant about 23% (unaffected by Pillar Two); GN about 22.6%. I model 17.0% (FY2027), 17.5% (FY2028 to FY2029), 18.0% (FY2030 to FY2031). Each point is about 1.2% of EPS; a move to 20% (the memo's sensitivity) would cost about 3.6% against my FY2030. DNB Carnegie's 15% (a September 2024 assumption) is out of date. Correction to the first set: the first set attributed four years of flat franc EPS to the franc alone; the tax rate removed about 9% of EPS between FY2023 (8.1%) and FY2026 (16.0%), so roughly a third of the "missing" franc EPS growth was tax, a headwind that does not recur.

Net financial items. History: CHF -24m to -35m a year (FactSet net interest -28.6m and -29.7m in FY2025 and FY2026; company net financial costs CHF -31.0m in H1 FY2026, including foreign-exchange losses). H1 FY2027: CHF -1.7m, helped by foreign-exchange results and valuation gains that I do not extrapolate. Model: CHF -14m (FY2027), then -22m, -23m, -24m, -25m (lease interest of about CHF 7m to 8m is included; interest income on rising cash is small at near-zero franc rates). Effect: FY2027 core EPS is about CHF 0.15 to 0.20 higher than a normal interest year would give; FY2028 EPS growth loses about 1.3 points as it normalises.

One-off items between core and reported. Restructuring, transaction and litigation costs: CHF 44m (FY2025), about CHF 87m of total adjustments between normalised and reported EBITA in FY2026 (including a CHF 34.7m software impairment, CHF 23.6m of cochlear product-liability provisions and CHF 28.2m of patent litigation in H1 FY2026), CHF 12.0m in H1 FY2027. Model: CHF -38m (FY2027), -30m (FY2028), -25m (FY2029), -20m (FY2030 to FY2031). Reported continuing EPS is therefore about CHF 0.53 below core in FY2027 and CHF 0.28 to 0.40 below thereafter. Discontinued Consumer Hearing: H1 FY2027 loss after tax CHF 56.8m (including a CHF 68.3m pre-tax impairment), about CHF 0.95 a share, hits total reported EPS in FY2027 only; sale completion is expected within FY2027 with small, undisclosed proceeds (I assume none).

Minorities and associates. Minorities about CHF 3m to 4m a year (balance-sheet minorities CHF 20.5m); associates CHF 10.3m on the balance sheet, negligible in the P&L. Immaterial.

Share count. 59.6m registered shares; about 59.5m ranked for the June 2026 dividend (CHF 279.8m at CHF 4.70). Flat since FY2023 because buybacks stopped after CHF 732m (FY2022) and CHF 486m (FY2023). Option exercises bring small cash (CHF 12.4m in H1 FY2027) and about 0.05m shares a year. Base case: buybacks of CHF 150m (FY2028) and CHF 300m a year (FY2029 to FY2031) at assumed prices of CHF 320 to 370, average shares 59.5m, 59.3m, 58.7m, 57.9m, 57.1m.

Net income and EPS, core basis. Core net income: about CHF 615m (FY2026), CHF 718m, 758m, 803m, 851m, 904m (FY2027 to FY2031). Core EPS: CHF 10.34, 12.07 (+16.7%), 12.77 (+5.8%), 13.68 (+7.1%), 14.69 (+7.4%), 15.82 (+7.7%). Bridge of the FY2026 to FY2031 core EPS CAGR of about 8.9%: operating growth in local currency about +10% a year, franc about -1.6, tax about -0.3, interest about -0.1 (the FY2027 benefit, then its reversal), buybacks about +0.8. The FY2028 growth rate is the most misleading number in the model: +5.8% reported core EPS growth contains a -1.6-point franc drag, a -1.3-point interest normalisation, a -0.6-point tax drift and the annualised investment step-up; underlying local-currency operating EPS growth is about 9% to 10%.

Free cash flow. History on three definitions (they differ and must not be mixed): FactSet (operating cash flow less fixed-asset capex) CHF 399m, 372m, 432m, 340m (FY2023 to FY2026), 62% to 77% of reported net income; the company's operating free cash flow (after all capex including intangibles, before lease principal) CHF 535.6m, 539.2m, 581.7m, 519.1m; my definition (after capex, lease principal and restructuring cash; before acquisitions, dividends and buybacks) about CHF 434m in FY2026. FY2026 was depressed by payables days falling from 94 to 60 and inventory building. Forecast on my definition: CHF 614m, 682m, 730m, 783m, 825m (about 86% to 91% of core net income); before lease payments (the consensus definition) CHF 705m, 777m, 830m, 888m, 935m against consensus CHF 684m, 773m, 867m, 934m. Drivers: EBITDA growth; working capital held at about 20% to 20.5% of revenue (inventory days normalising from 187 toward about 165 after the EON build); capex rising from CHF 103m to CHF 140m to 172m (3.6% to 3.7% of sales); lease principal CHF 90m to 110m as the clinic network grows; cash tax at 17% to 18%.

C-78

Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

B648th of 38

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: plausible scope of a further 150 to 250 basis points of core EBIT margin and about 1 point a year of extra growth by FY2030, coming from four big areas; precedents (Sonova's own FY2022 margin of 23.3% on FactSet's basis; Demant's KIND conversion; Amplifon's Fit4Growth) show it is achievable; it is congruent with the stated strategy.

The four big areas.

  1. Cochlear implant recovery: segment margin from 6.8% back toward 11.4% on CHF 250m to 300m of revenue, plus about 1 point of group growth from Integro at full commercialisation: about +15 to 25 basis points of group margin.
  2. Mix toward wholesale and premium: wholesale (above 30% EBIT) growing faster than retail (mid-teens); EON and an instant-fit custom device lifting premium share: about +30 to 60 basis points.
  3. Retail productivity: density from bolt-ons, internal lead generation, flagship stores (twice average sales), EasyGuard service savings; but customer acquisition costs are rising: 0 to +50 basis points of retail margin, about 0 to +20 at group.
  4. Working capital and cash: inventory from 187 days back to about 160 days frees about CHF 50m to 70m; capital redeployed to buybacks if M&A stays small.

Plus many small items: Asia scale, procurement (+2 to 3 points of core EBIT growth per Morgan Stanley), digital (SilentCloud, remote care), Costco efficiency.

Congruence. All four map onto the three pillars and the margin bridge (+2 to 3 footprint, +1 to 2 leverage, minus 1 to 3 investment). The incongruent item is larger retail M&A, which raises growth and lowers margin.

C-80

To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

B668th of 38

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: no deceleration over the last twelve months; the engine accelerated (local-currency growth 4.9% in H1 FY2026, 5.9% in FY2026, 7.5% in H1 FY2027; wholesale 9.5% to 11.0%), above Sonova's normal 5% to 6% organic rate, and the input force (market growth) also accelerated. A deceleration is, however, built into H2 FY2027 guidance and into the arithmetic of annualising tailwinds.

Evidence. H1 FY2027 organic about 7%; wholesale +11% (consensus 9.5%); retail organic 5.2%; Asia-Pacific +11.4%; market growth raised to 3% to 5%; Q3 calendar 2026 market value +6% to 7%; VA purchases +7% to 8% year on year. Only Cochlear Implants decelerated (-11.1% FY2026, -10.2% H1 FY2027).

The forward deceleration. Guidance midpoint implies H2 wholesale about 5% (Morgan Stanley) and group about 6% to 7% local currency; Virto R annualises in November 2026, Costco in March 2027; the August 2026 launch spike in the market will annualise in H2 calendar 2027; Amplifon's phase-out starts in calendar 2027. Jefferies' bear case (tailwinds annualising into crowded launches) is about this period.

Monitoring thresholds (from the research). Wholesale growth below 5% local currency would signal demand weakness; VA total share below 50% for a month is an alarm and below 48% for two months invalidates the share-gain thesis; custom share below 40% would signal competitive pressure.

How do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five years, for the main financial statement line items in particular and the granular working up to them, and how credible is the gap given the change forces and foundations found above?

The Investment Analyst's heading How do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five years, and how credible is the gap given the change forces and foundations?

Judgement: consensus forecasts a clear break from the last five years in francs (revenue +6% to 8% a year against -3% to 0%; EPS +10% to 12% a year against flat), but not in local currency; the operating assumptions (local-currency growth, margin expansion) are credible and possibly conservative for FY2027; the gap's fragile element is the implicit assumption that the franc stops eroding reported results after FY2027.

Line by line (FactSet consensus median against FactSet history, francs).

  • Revenue: FY2024 to FY2026 -3.0%, -0.4%, -0.2%; consensus FY2027 to FY2030 +7.8%, +7.4%, +6.5%, +6.4%. Local-currency history was +3% to +7%; consensus is therefore local-currency growth of 6% to 8% with zero FX. Segment consensus: wholesale CHF 2,011m, 2,139m, 2,268m (+6% a year after FY2027); retail CHF 1,603m, 1,726m, 1,849m (+7% to 8% a year, including bolt-ons); implants CHF 259m, 279m, 296m (+3%, +8%, +6%).
  • Margins: FactSet adjusted EBIT margin 19.1% to 21.3% (FY2024 to FY2026); consensus 22.1%, 22.7%, 23.2%, 23.8% (FY2027 to FY2030, brokers' basis). EBITDA margin 27.3% (FY2026, brokers' basis) to 27.6%, 28.2%, 28.8%, 29.5%. About 50 to 60 basis points a year, inside the company's margin bridge.
  • EPS: brokers' basis 10.06, 10.81, 10.34 (FY2024 to FY2026); consensus 11.55, 12.80, 14.05, 15.13 (+11.7%, +10.9%, +9.8%, +7.6%); FY2030 rests on five brokers.
  • Cash: consensus free cash flow CHF 684m to 934m (FactSet definitions differ from history); capex CHF 141m to 162m (about 3.6% of sales against about 2.8%); dividend CHF 4.98 to 5.63; total debt flat at about CHF 1.5bn (no buybacks or large M&A assumed).

Credibility. Local-currency growth: credible (H1 7.5%; market 3% to 5%; EON), though FY2028 to FY2029 must absorb Amplifon (about 0.5 points a year) and annualisation. Margin: credible (H1 +250 basis points; CHF 90m programme; mix). FX: the weak point; house forecasts (Maybank USD/CHF to 0.80; Morgan Stanley EUR/CHF toward 0.87) imply renewed franc strength of 2% to 6%, worth 2% to 5% of EBIT a year if realised. EPS FY2028 consensus (CHF 12.80) is modest relative to an updated FY2027 of CHF 12.0 to 12.3, so FY2028 can absorb some franc drag. Not in consensus: buybacks (upside 1% to 2% a year), larger M&A, tax drift (downside 2% to 5%).

P-09

After assessing the other questions/items in the set in greater detail, what is the net likely impact to the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

A583rd of 26

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: the forecasts describe a clear break from the last four to six years in francs (revenue growth about 5% a year against about 2%, a core margin up 260 basis points against six flat years, EPS growth about 9% a year against zero, free cash flow nearly doubling) but almost no break in local-currency operations (growth about 6% against 5% to 6%). Three of the four drivers of the break are the removal of headwinds (the franc drag shrinking from about 4 to 5 points a year to about 1, the tax step-up completed, Mexicali ramp-up costs gone), not new strengths. The forecast is therefore concentrated in one external driver, the franc, and that is what the third set must test.

How concentrated the difference is (for D-03). Of the roughly +9 points a year of CHF EPS growth I forecast against the last four years' zero: about 3.5 points are the franc drag shrinking (from about 5 points of EBIT a year to 1.6), about 2 points the tax step-up not recurring, about 1 point margin expansion beyond FY2027, about 1.5 points the FY2027 ramp-cost relief and gross-margin reset averaged over five years, about 0.8 point buybacks, and the rest local-currency growth that was always there. The franc assumption alone carries about 40% of the difference. Each additional point of annual franc appreciation beyond my assumption removes about 1.6% of EBIT and EPS a year, about 5% by FY2030; a drag of 3 points a year on sales (about 5 on EBIT, still only about half the FY2024 to FY2026 average) would leave FY2030 EPS at about CHF 13.2 to 13.5 rather than 14.7.

What does not change. Local-currency organic growth of 5% to 6% with 1 point of bolt-ons, wholesale as the profit engine (above 30% margins), Cochlear Implants as a small, volatile segment, and an earnings base with a permanent core-to-reported gap of CHF 20m to 40m a year.

The fourth set as a whole

Layer 4 · The fourth set as a whole

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Our assessment is largely reflected in guidance, in sell-side estimates and in the narrative for the next six months, and is slightly less favourable than what they reflect for months 6 to 18. The gap that favours an investment case lies further out and depends on one decision by management.

Next six months. There is little gap. FY2027 guidance is conservative, but consensus sits at its top and so do we; the result on 18 May 2027 should be as expected. Revision breadth will look positive into the new year for a mechanical reason. Nothing in the company's calendar can raise guidance before May.

Months 6 to 18. The gap turns mildly unfavourable. The FY2028 guide is likely to sit below consensus in francs (about three chances in four); FY2028 consensus is about 2.5% above our forecast with only about one chance in three of being reached; and the next two half-year prints are likely to show core EPS growth of about nil and about 2%, after 36%. The sector has paid for upgrades, not for cheapness, and upgrades from operations are unlikely before November 2027 at the earliest. The dynamics that no one has written about all bear on that print.

Eighteen months to three years. We are 5% to 7% below consensus before deployment and about level with it after: consensus for FY2029 and FY2030 is, in effect, our forecast plus a neutral franc, plus about one point a year of extra growth, minus the deployment it does not include. Whether estimates rise depends on whether management spends CHF 0.6bn to 0.9bn, on what, and at what price.

For the memo's topics.

  • Conservative guidance and upgrades (topics 4, 7 and 8): guidance remains conservative, but it has been raised to where the Street already was. Momentum in upgrades is real over the last six months (+9% for FY2028 EPS) and is ending in substance; what continues is the catch-up of stale estimates.
  • Life cycle (topic 10): late in the reinforcing phase, with the first evidence of whether it stalls due in November 2027.
  • Fair multiple and return (topics 11 and 12): the guideline fair multiple of 23.81 times is above the 75th percentile of the ten-year range (23.1 times) and presumes peak delivery lasting through March 2028. The evidence in these two sets fits a multiple between the ten-year median (20.7 times) and today's (about 21.6 to 21.9 times) better, because the condition for a peak multiple, continuing upgrades, is what the next two prints put in doubt; 23.8 times would need an event consensus does not carry. On the model as it will stand after the input changes (EPS of about CHF 14.55 at the 18-month column), the return to 31 March 2028 with one dividend is about +20% at the guideline multiple, of which about half is the re-rating; about +10% at an unchanged multiple; about +5% at the ten-year median; and about -23% at the ten-year tenth percentile (16.8 times) on EPS 10% lower. The model's printed 18-month figure also counts a second dividend (about 1.9 points) that falls after the horizon.

I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

B542nd of 12

Layer 4 · Guidance

The Investment Analyst's heading How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events, and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, the mid-term targets.

Judgement: there are three events in the next 18 months (18 May 2027, mid-November 2027, mid-May 2028). At the first, FY2027 guidance will almost certainly be met at its top or slightly exceeded, which consensus already assumes, and the first FY2028 guide will most likely be cautious and, once put into francs, below consensus. At the second, a raise is much less likely than it was in November 2026 (about one chance in five), a cut about as likely as a raise. The mid-term ranges are wide enough to be kept; the CHF 6bn ambition will not be reached without a large acquisition. Guidance is slightly conservative against our forecast for FY2027, and the FY2028 guide we expect would be about level with our forecast, so guidance is no longer a likely source of surprise in the company's favour.

Event 1, 18 May 2027: the FY2027 outcome. Guidance is sales +6% to 8% in local currencies and core EBIT +10% to 13% in constant currency (CHF 839m to 862m). My base is +7.4% and about CHF 864m.

  • Sales: above 8% about 20%; within the range about 78%; below about 2%.
  • Core EBIT: above the top about 50%; within the range about 47%; below about 3%.
  • What would put it above the top: second-half wholesale growth of 8% or more (Costco is incremental for five of the six months; the premium Sphere model is about two-thirds of EON units, Bernstein), and an investment step-up at the low end of "low double-digit millions".
  • What would keep it within: the second half of FY2026 is a hard base (gross margin already above 75%; operating costs grew only 1% in that half, DNB Carnegie), the step-up, Amplifon's shift beginning, and Integro not yet approved in the US.
  • A formal raise before May is unlikely (about 10%): there is no scheduled event, though Sonova did raise between results in February 2020.

Event 1: the first FY2028 guide. The new team's one precedent (May 2026) was a guide below what brokers then expected, with a low market assumption and a stated franc drag, raised six months later. I expect the same shape: sales +5% to 7% in local currencies (possibly 5% to 8%) and core EBIT +7% to 10% in constant currency, with the franc effect stated at early-May spot rates. My base for FY2028 (+6.3% and about +8.5%) sits in the upper middle of such a guide. Against consensus:

  • Consensus FY2028 is revenue of CHF 4,169m and EBIT of CHF 947m: growth of 7.3% and about 9% to 10% in francs, with no currency effect.
  • If the franc is where it is today, a 5% to 7% and 7% to 10% guide equals CHF 4,060m to 4,140m and CHF 925m to 950m: consensus is at or above the top.
  • If the franc follows the banks' published paths (EUR/CHF 0.89 to 0.915 by mid-2027), the guide would state a drag of one to two points on sales and two to three on core EBIT, as in May 2026, and equals CHF 3,985m to 4,100m and CHF 900m to 935m: wholly below consensus.
  • I put the chance that the guide's midpoint, in francs, is below consensus at about 75%. The market's reading of 18 May 2027 is therefore likely to be "FY2027 as expected, FY2028 guided below the Street", softened by the team's reputation for guiding low.
  • Capital allocation at the same event: a buyback announcement about 25%; a larger acquisition announced by then about 20% to 25%; neither about 50% to 55%.

Event 2, November 2027: first half of FY2028. Raise about 20%, confirm about 62%, cut about 18%. In the six guided years before FY2027 the company raised mid-year once (FY2020), cut once (FY2023) and held four times; November 2026 was a raise because the first guide had been set for a weak market and a strong franc and neither arrived. In November 2027 the half reported is the hardest of the cycle (D-03).

  • A raise needs: wholesale growth of about 7% or more against the +11% comparison, which requires EON to take share in independents or a successful instant-fit custom device, and a recovering implant business.
  • A cut needs: a lost launch window (VA value share below 50%), a stall in US private-pay demand, a tariff, or Amplifon leaving faster while the higher cost base stays.
  • The franc is not in constant-currency guidance, so it cannot cause a formal cut; it does its damage in the reported figures and in consensus (I-02).

Event 3, May 2028: the FY2028 outcome and the FY2029 guide. Profit against the initial range: above about 15%, within about 65%, below about 20%. The base rate before the new team is two profit outcomes below the initial range in six years (FY2023 and FY2024), each in a year when a US channel weakened. The FY2029 guide should again be set low; a platform due about August 2028 gives it cover.

Mid-term (set March 2026): sales growth of 5% to 10% a year in local currencies, core EBIT growth of 7% to 12% a year in constant currency, CHF 6bn of revenue by FY2030/31. My forecasts are about 6.2% and about 9%, inside both ranges. Kept as they are about 75%; narrowed or lowered about 20%; raised about 5%. The peers have been resetting to "5% to 7%" (Demant on 24-Nov-26, from 6% to 8%; Coloplast, from 7% to 8%), and Sonova's range, which includes one to two points of acquisitions, is already consistent with that. The CHF 6bn ambition needs about CHF 1.1bn of acquired revenue on my numbers (Jefferies: about CHF 0.8bn). Brokers give it no credit, so dropping it would cost little; pursuing it with a large retail acquisition is the event to watch, because it would raise EPS and lower margin and return on capital together.

Layer 4 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: for about three months the breadth of revisions will stay positive, but mechanically: stale FY2027 estimates catching up with a result already published. Beyond that the direction turns flat to down. FY2027 consensus is level with our forecast, so the May 2027 print should not move it. FY2028 consensus (EPS of CHF 12.84) is about 2.5% above our forecast and is more likely to be trimmed than raised at the May and November 2027 events. FY2029 and FY2030 consensus is 5% to 7% above our forecast and will fall unless the franc stays neutral or the balance sheet is deployed. The most likely cause of upgrades in the next 18 months is an acquisition, which no estimate includes.

FY2027: catching up, then level. The FactSet median was CHF 11.55 on 14-Nov-26 and CHF 11.80 in the model on 22-Nov-26; the mean is CHF 11.44. Six of the estimates behind it are stale or on another basis (RBC 10.16, Oddo 10.45, Vontobel 10.62, Barclays 10.74, Equita 11.49, UBS 11.55); the brokers who have updated sit at CHF 11.56 to 12.25 (Morgan Stanley 11.89, Jefferies 12.00, Bernstein 12.25). As the laggards refresh, the mean rises about 4% to 5%. Revision screens will show this as upgrades through January; it carries no information. My FY2027 EPS of about CHF 11.8 (CHF 12.0 if the second-half financial result repeats the first half's) is level with the updated brokers, and my core EBIT of CHF 864m is level with consensus (CHF 859m to 868m). The one line likely to miss in May is Cochlear Implants: consensus of CHF 259m for the year needs the second half to grow about 18%, with Integro launched only in Europe from October and no FDA date; I have CHF 243m. The profit at stake is small (CHF 5m to 8m), but it would be the third miss running in that segment.

FY2028: where our forecast and consensus part.

FY2028 Consensus (FactSet, in the model) Ours Difference
Revenue 4,169 4,074 -2.3%
EBITDA 1,179 1,149 -2.5%
EBIT 947 924 -2.4%

Margins are the same (22.7%). The whole difference is revenue: one point of franc and one point of local-currency growth (consensus 7.3%, ours 6.3%), found in retail and implants more than in wholesale. Consensus is, in effect, Morgan Stanley's model: organic growth of 6.5% plus bolt-ons and no currency effect.

  • Cut (about 40% that FY2028 consensus EPS is more than 2% lower in twelve months): the May guide in francs, a franc at or below 0.90 to the euro, first-half growth near 5% in November.
  • Held (about 40% within 2% either way): a franc near today's rates and wholesale growth of 6% to 7%.
  • Raised (about 20% more than 2% higher): an acquisition or a sizeable buyback; a franc weaker than today; EON taking share in independents.
  • Expected drift: about -1% to -3% over twelve months, and it will arrive at the two events, not gradually.

Why EBITDA revisions turned negative (-2% in a month) while EPS revisions stayed positive (+1%). The AlphaSense report attributes it to currency translation and the add-back of discontinued losses. With the franc neutral for FY2027, I think it is mostly definitional: after the first-half report brokers rebuilt their models on the continuing, core basis, with lower depreciation and amortisation (FactSet's total fell from CHF 258m in FY2025 to CHF 226m in FY2026 and CHF 98m in the first half of FY2027) and a lower financial charge, so the same EBIT and a higher EPS sit on a lower EBITDA. It is not a sign of weakening operations. Read EBIT.

Ratings and targets. The buy share is 17% (down ten points in a month), the mean rating is Hold and the mean target is CHF 283 against a price of about CHF 291; targets have risen 14% in a month and 35% in six. Targets are following the price. Rating changes are now two-way (Jefferies up to Hold on 10-Nov-26; Barclays reported to have gone to Overweight on 18-Nov-26, though FactSet still showed Sell on 12-Nov-26). From 17% the buy share has room to rise, but a broker needs higher estimates to justify it, and I do not see where those come from before November 2027.

FY2029 and FY2030 (secondary). Consensus EPS is CHF 14.34 and 15.18; ours is about CHF 13.4 and 14.4. Consensus holds debt flat at about CHF 1.5bn (no buyback, no acquisition), assumes no franc drag and grows sales about 6.4% a year. The gap closes in one of three ways: consensus comes down (three points of cumulative franc drag and 2.4 points of slower growth by FY2030); our forecast goes up through deployment (an acquisition of CHF 0.7bn to 1bn adds 5% to 7% to EPS, buybacks 2% to 3%); or both. I put the chance that FY2029 consensus EPS is higher in twelve months at about 30% and lower at about 50%.

The paradox to carry into the memo. The event most likely to produce upgrades is a larger acquisition (about 40% by May 2028): brokers will add its revenue and earnings the day it is announced. It is also the event the first set scored as a risk, because it dilutes margin and return on capital and tests an unproven allocator. Upgrades of that kind are worth less to the multiple than upgrades from operations.

11d

Current multiple

–not yet ratedMemo topic 11, : the Rater's score1 answer
D-0754
D-07

To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

C+544th of 12

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: mostly company-driven. Over the latest six-month window the peer basket explains 39% of Sonova's daily price variance and Sonova's own factors 61%; of the six-month return of about +47%, roughly 20 points are what its beta to the basket would have given and roughly 27 points are its own. The multiple is more company-specific still: Sonova's price-to-earnings ratio stands at the 60th percentile of its ten-year range while the median peer stands at about the 11th and its closest peer, Demant, at the 33rd. Two qualifications matter: the link to the sector is at a two-year high and rising, and a multiple that is company-driven has no sector support beneath it.

The price (co-movement table, 24-Nov-26).

  • Latest six months (2-Jun-26 to 24-Nov-26): correlation with the equal-weighted basket of 17 priced peers 0.62, beta 0.93, R-squared 39%. Sonova +46.9%, basket +21.8%.
  • Over 24 months the R-squared has ranged from 8% (February 2025) to 44% (September 2025), averaging about a quarter. The present 39% equals the second-highest reading, and the beta has risen from about 0.6 in the spring of 2026 to 0.93 to 1.00 in October and November. As the shares re-rated they became more, not less, a part of the European medical-technology recovery trade.
  • Industry, as distinct from sector: the correlation with Demant is 0.48 (R-squared 23%), the highest of any single peer but low for two companies selling the same products into the same market. The hearing-aid industry factor explains less than a quarter of Sonova's daily moves. The two shares are driven by each company's own prints and by the monthly VA data, in which one's gain is often the other's loss.

The multiple.

  • The 24-month-forward price-to-earnings ratio rose from about 16 to 18 times in May 2026 to 21.2 times on 13-Nov-26 (21.9 times in the model on 22-Nov-26). Consensus FY2028 EPS rose 9% over the same six months. About two-thirds of the price rise was therefore the multiple and about one-third earnings and the passage of time.
  • The sector did not re-rate: the median of 21 peers sits at about the 11th percentile of its own ten-year range and only two (Fresenius, Getinge) sit above Sonova's 60th. So Sonova's higher multiple is not a sector effect.
  • Nor is it mainly a hearing-industry effect. Demant rose 51% in twelve months on larger upgrades (the dashboard rates its earnings momentum A, Sonova's C) and trades at 17.6 times against Sonova's 21.6 times: a premium of about 23%, against the 10% to 15% the first set cites as the historical premium for Sonova's steadier growth. Morgan Stanley's target multiples put Demant (21.5 times) above Sonova (21 times).
  • What the market is paying for is therefore specific to Sonova: the consumer exit, the margin recovery, the franc turning neutral, the new team's two beats and the lowest earnings volatility in the group.

What it means.

  1. The return on the case depends on Sonova's own two prints (May and November 2027) and the monthly VA series far more than on the sector. That suits a thesis built on company analysis.
  2. The same fact removes a cushion. With the sector near the bottom of its range, a disappointment at Sonova would not be softened by a sector that is itself expensive and falling; the shares would move toward peers' multiples, not with them. The premium to Demant is the most exposed part.
  3. Sector risk has not gone: at a beta near 1 and a correlation at a two-year high, a rotation out of medical technology would now carry Sonova with it more than at any point in the last two years.

Outside the eleven ratings

Answers not placed under any of the eleven ratings

The table the ratings map uses places these nowhere; each is shown with the reason the table records.

Why it is not placed It asks whether the stock fits the sector's historical patterns of share price and financial outperformance, a cross-check across many topics rather than one of the eleven.

C-84

What do historical precedents, rules of thumb and the empirical evidence show about how companies in this sector and industry have typically generated share price, financial and operational outperformance, and underperformance, and to what extent does this company fit those patterns?

C+5510th of 38

Layer 1 · Industry and Value Chain

The Investment Analyst's heading What do historical precedents, rules of thumb and empirical evidence show about how companies in this sector and industry outperform and underperform, and to what extent does this company fit those patterns?

Judgement: in hearing aids, share prices outperform when a maker wins a product cycle (share gains plus margin expansion plus estimate upgrades) from a depressed multiple, and underperform when growth slows, a profit warning lands, the franc rises or a rival's launch wins; Sonova fits the outperformance pattern on fundamentals today but has already been priced for it, which historically is when the pattern turns.

Precedents and rules.

  • Product cycles drive re-ratings: Marvel (2018), Paradise (2020), Lumity (2022) and Infinio (2024) each lifted Sonova's wholesale growth; the market extrapolates launches into multiple expansion (Morgan Stanley); a launch premium lasts as long as the technical lead, 12 to 18 months, and a crowded window caps it (Sector Digest).
  • "Slow growth is punished twice": the 2021 to mid-2026 de-rating from 28.5x (calendar 2021) to about 16x came from slowing growth, a profit warning (August 2022: adjusted EBITA growth cut from 12%-18% to 6%-10%), the loss of a large US customer (2023 to 2024), the Sennheiser drag and FX.
  • In European medtech, multiples rebound through multiple expansion after a sell-off as worries clear (Morgan Stanley); in the six months to November 2026 the sector paid for earnings upgrades, not cheapness (rank correlation 0.68 between price change and revision); "buy a known story after a fall, not after its rise" (the Sector Digest's call on hearing aid makers).
  • Earnings volatility sets the premium: Sonova's lower growth volatility (3.0 against Demant's 4.5 standard deviation) has historically earned a 10% to 15% premium to Demant.
  • Demant's 2024 to 2025 sequence shows the downside pattern: guidance cut four times in two years, a 22x multiple de-rated.

Fit. Sonova re-rated from about 16x to 21.2x two-year-forward earnings between May and November 2026 (63rd percentile of five years, 80th of two years), price +46.9% over twelve months, relative strength 94th percentile, while the median Medtech consumables stock sits at the 14th percentile of its own range. The worries have cleared (market recovery, Consumer exit, EON launched), estimates are rising (+9% over six months for FY2028 EPS), and the stock is at a 52-week high. That is the late stage of the pattern: further outperformance needs further upgrades, which depend on winning the launch window and on the franc.

Why it is not placed The table holds this question in reserve for Riskiness sub-items that are not yet built, so it is not yet placed.

C-83

How far could the shares fall to a fair trough multiple?

F3033rd of 38

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: to a fair trough multiple on unchanged earnings, about 20% to 25% (to about CHF 220 to 230); with a moderate earnings disappointment, about 30% to 35% (CHF 190 to 205); in a severe combined case, 40% to 45%.

Inputs. Price CHF 290.6 (18-Nov-26); 24-month-forward P/E 21.17x (FactSet), implying 24-month-forward EPS of about CHF 13.7. Ten-year bands: 16.8x (10th percentile, "crash"), 19.4x (35th), 20.7x (median), 23.1x (75th); five-year range 14.3x to 28.4x; brokers' bear multiples 13x to 14x (Jefferies, Morgan Stanley, set at a lower price base in mid-2026). The memo's re-entry levels: about CHF 227 (eighteen-month horizon) and about CHF 200 (both horizons).

Scenarios (my calculations).

  • Multiple to 16.8x on unchanged EPS of CHF 13.7: CHF 230 (-21%).
  • Multiple to 16.8x with EPS 10% lower (launch window lost, franc drag resumes): CHF 207 (-29%).
  • Multiple to the five-year low of 14.3x with EPS 10% lower: CHF 176 (-39%).
  • Severe (franc spike plus tariff plus share loss, EPS 15% lower, 14x): CHF 163 (-44%).

Why 16.8x is the fair trough. It is the stock's ten-year 10th percentile and close to the May 2026 level (16x to 18x) from which it re-rated; the business is now better (Consumer exit, higher margin, lower leverage), which argues against the 13x to 14x bear multiples except in a joint fundamental-and-market shock.

Why it is not placed It rates the staircase of news the market can look forward to, and none of the eleven rates the narrative or the catalysts as such.

Group judgement

Stock market narrative

B605th of 38

Layer 1 · Stock market narrative

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The narrative ahead is a continuation story with dated tests rather than a sequence of novel surprises: monthly VA data and the May 2027 FY2028 guide are the near-term steps; capital allocation (buyback or acquisition), the instant-fit custom launch and Integro's US approval are the novel steps in 6 to 18 months; the next platform around August 2028 is the step beyond. Because the shares already price a won launch window, the narrative needs the novel steps (especially a buyback or a margin beat) to add to the price; the known steps can only confirm it. For the fourth set's judgement, the key dates are the monthly VA releases, 24 November 2026 (Demant), 18 May 2027 (FY2027 results and FY2028 guidance) and November 2027 (H1 FY2028).

Why it is not placed It rates how well the earlier answers agree with each other, which is a check on the analysis rather than on the company.

D-01

How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets: to what extent do they support and align with each other, rather than contradict or jar?

–64

Layer 3 · Risks

The Investment Analyst's heading How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets?

Judgement: highly congruent in structure and direction, with three places where the sets jar. One was an arithmetic base that I have now corrected, and its correction removes the earlier sets' most comfortable conclusion (that our forecast sits above guidance and level with consensus). Two are judgements the sets left unresolved: how much franc drag to assume, and what the base case should assume about the balance sheet.

What aligns, and bears the weight of the case. Both sets, written independently, reach the same five conclusions. (1) The operating engine is steady: local-currency growth of about 6% to 7% and rising margins, changing little from the past. (2) The break from the last four years is in reported francs, and it depends on headwinds ending (franc, tax, Mexicali ramp-up costs) more than on new strengths. (3) FY2027 is close to secured: the first half is reported and guidance is conservative. (4) The above-market growth is finite: Virto R, Costco and the VA gains annualise by March 2027, share gains fade within one to two platform cycles, and Amplifon's purchases leave. (5) Cash outruns its uses, so a deployment decision is forced. The industry, moat, economics and management answers support these without strain: an oligopoly with price discipline explains stable price; a moat that must be re-won every two years explains the fade; the mixed five-year delivery record and the new team's two beats explain "conservative guidance, modest beat".

Where they jar, in order of consequence.

  1. The core EBIT base (correction 1 above). The second set's FY2027 was three points above guidance while it said "top of guidance", and its "margin above consensus" was an artefact. Corrected, the forecast is at the top of guidance and level with consensus on margin, and below consensus on revenue and EPS from FY2028. The first set's remark that FY2028 consensus "is not demanding" no longer holds on our own numbers.
  2. The franc. Both sets call it the decisive variable and both then choose the mildest non-zero assumption (one point a year on sales). Sixteen years of history average about two and a half to three points a year; with Swiss interest rates at zero, forward exchange rates imply a drag of similar size (my estimate); and neutral or better years have been about one in three. The assumption is defensible as a mid-point between consensus (zero) and history, but the sets' own emphasis argues that the risk around it is one-sided. I keep the input and treat the asymmetry in D-04.
  3. The balance sheet. The second set shows that management prefers acquisitions and that an acquisition adds more EPS per franc than a buyback at today's price, then puts a buyback in the base case "as a proxy". Since then management has ruled out a buyback in the second half of FY2027 because of "a strong pipeline of larger M&A opportunities" (Bernstein, 16-Nov-26; company presentation, September 2026). The base-case input is therefore the less likely of the two forms of deployment. It is small in the next 18 months (about 0.3% of FY2028 EPS), so I leave it, but the memo should not describe a buyback as the expected event.
  4. Smaller points. (a) Tax: the second set says the step-up is "largely complete", yet the transitional safe-harbour relief under the minimum-tax rules ends on 31 March 2027, so FY2028 is the first year without it; the company has said 17% to 18% (May 2024), which the 17.5% input covers. (b) Amplifon: the sets phase the loss evenly over FY2028 to FY2030; Jefferies reports management expecting the shift to begin in the second half of FY2027, and Rothschild & Co Redburn expects Amplifon to move from 40% to 90% in-house within 18 to 24 months of closing (end-2026), which front-loads the loss into FY2028 (perhaps CHF 25m to 35m against the CHF 15m to 20m modelled). (c) The chief executive's record: the first set counted a fall in gross margin at Sivantos (59.8% to 56.8%) against him, but the source is dated 1 March 2019, so it describes Sivantos before the merger and before his tenure (2019 to 2024); the only dated criticism of his time at WS Audiology is slow integration, which Bernstein attributes partly to active owners. (d) Wholesale in FY2028: the first set expected +6% to 8%, the second uses +5.5%; I keep the second (the arithmetic after Costco and Virto R annualise supports 5% to 6.5%).

What the pattern of scores says. The earlier sets score the business well (industry 64, moat 66, economics 62, management 60, internal change 66) and the price-side risks poorly (sentiment 36, fall to trough 30). That is not a contradiction; it is the case in one line: good fundamentals that the share price already reflects.

Why it is not placed It rates how well the sell-side and investors already articulate the case, and none of the eleven rates the narrative or how much of the case is known.

I-03

How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary (for example on Twitter): how well known is each of them to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years out?

D428th of 12

Layer 4 · Stock market narrative

The Investment Analyst's heading How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of sell-side and investor commentary, and how well known is each?

Judgement: the favourable dynamics are fully articulated and priced. The unfavourable ones are articulated in words but mostly absent from the numbers. A small group of dynamics is discussed by no one, and for the next 18 months these lean negative for reported earnings; the one favourable dynamic that is known but not in any estimate is the deployment of the balance sheet. On investor commentary, as distinct from brokers, I have no direct evidence.

Retail customer acquisition costs Jefferies, Redburn, Bernstein Known
Integro, the FDA and the implant recovery Jefferies, Morgan Stanley, Bernstein; "overshadowed" by hearing aids Known and small; consensus for FY2027 implants looks too high
Section 232 tariff Redburn (160 basis points of gross margin at 25%), Morgan Stanley (2% of EBIT), DNB Carnegie Known as a tail risk; in no estimate

Three observations.

  1. What is not in the price is not the bear case in words, which every Hold-rated broker has written; it is the bear case in numbers. The estimates assume a neutral franc, growth above 7% in FY2028 and no tax or financing drag. The mean rating is Hold and yet the shares trade above the mean target at the 60th percentile of their ten-year multiple: the marginal buyer is following price momentum (relative strength at the 94th percentile) and upgrades, not the brokers.
  2. The unarticulated items are individually small and arrive together. The tax rate after the safe harbour, the financial result returning to normal, the cost step-up annualising and the new presentation standard all take effect from 1 April 2027 and all show in the November 2027 print. None is in any note I have seen. Together they explain why first-half FY2028 EPS grows about 2% on my base when core EBIT grows 6%.
  3. The favourable item is known and unpriced. Capacity of about CHF 0.3bn to 0.5bn at March 2027, rising to CHF 0.6bn to 0.9bn by March 2028 (the amounts that would return leverage to 1.0x to 1.25x), is discussed by every broker and included by none. For 18 months to three years out this is the largest thing our assessment contains that the estimates do not, together with the savings programme reaching full rate and the platform due about August 2028.

Limits of the evidence. The AlphaSense narrative report rests on Morgan Stanley, Bernstein, Jefferies, DNB Carnegie, Deutsche Bank and Rothschild & Co Redburn, with Barclays at second hand; it does not state its coverage, and it has nothing from Goldman Sachs (Buy, target CHF 325), UBS, RBC, Oddo BHF, Vontobel or Zurcher Kantonalbank. The two highest targets on FactSet (CHF 330 and CHF 325) are therefore unrepresented, so the bull narrative may be better articulated than this answer can show. It contains no fund letters, positioning data or social-media commentary; the only positioning fact in the files is short interest above 19% of the free float in late 2025 (Bernstein), which a 47% rise has presumably reduced. I have asked for this in the research requests.

Layer 1: Assessing the foundational components of an investment case

Written 19 November 2026, 15:44 to 16:01 UK, Opus, ricardinho login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to investigate and research each question, analyse the relevant information and form an insightful judgement regarding it, and to do the same for each group of questions as a whole, so as to best help the Consumer achieve their objective, which is to produce a well-reasoned, coherent, deeply understood and accurate operating model and financial model for the company for the next 18 months to four years.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Sonova (SOON-CH): First set of questions, foundational components of the investment case

Written 2026-11-19 by the Investment Analyst answering the first set, for the Investment Analyst who builds the operating and financial model (second set).

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Before the answers: source check, basis traps and the anchor numbers

Source check (the caution in the instruction). The latest Earnings Review does cover the period FactSet shows as the latest reported: Sonova's H1 FY2026/27, the six months to 30 September 2026, reported on 12 November 2026 (the review quotes the 12-Nov-26 earnings release, presentation, call and the brokers' notes of 12 to 16 November; FactSet's last period in the financials block is 09/2026, H1-FY2027, revenue CHF 1,834m, matching the review's CHF 1,834.1m). The earlier review covers FY2025/26 (to 31 March 2026, reported 18 May 2026). Both can be relied upon.

Basis traps the Consumer must respect when building the model.

  • Sonova changed its headline profit metric in March 2026 from normalised (adjusted) EBITA to core EBIT. Core EBIT includes amortisation of acquired intangibles (purchase price allocation) and excludes restructuring and transaction costs (guided at CHF 35m to 40m for FY2027). History before FY2027 is mostly quoted as normalised EBITA (FY2026 CHF 811.2m, 22.5% margin). The restated FY2026 core EBIT base is not given in the files; by my reconstruction (H1 FY2026 core EBIT CHF 316.4m restated, plus H2 normalised EBITA CHF 480.0m less roughly CHF 10m to 20m of acquisition amortisation) it is approximately CHF 775m to 790m. The Consumer should verify this from the 12-Nov-26 release before using it as the base for the 10% to 13% guidance.
  • Consumer Hearing (Sennheiser) is in discontinued operations from March 2026; all continuing figures exclude it. FY2026 continuing revenue CHF 3,605.9m.
  • EPS comes on four bases. FactSet consensus FY2027 EPS of CHF 11.55 (median) mixes stale and different-basis estimates (RBC 10.16, Oddo 10.45, Vontobel 10.62, Barclays 10.74). The post-results updated brokers sit at CHF 11.56 (Deutsche Bank) to 12.60 (DNB Carnegie), cluster 11.9 to 12.25 (Morgan Stanley 11.89, Jefferies 12.00, Bernstein 12.25). H1 FY2027 core EPS alone was CHF 5.37.
  • Wholesale market shares differ by source: Bernstein (2025) Sonova 25.3%, WS Audiology 23.5%, Demant 21.5%, GN 17.0%, Starkey 6.8%; Jefferies (November 2026) Sonova 29%, WS Audiology 25%, Demant 19%, GN 13%. US Veterans Affairs (VA) share is quoted both by units (54.0% July 2026) and by value (53.5% September 2026).
  • Leverage: company 1.1x net debt to EBITDA at end-September 2026; FactSet 1.01x; one broker quotes 0.7x (unreconciled, likely a different debt definition).
  • Sell-side rating mix: FactSet buy share 17% (dashboard, 19-Nov-26) against Refinitiv 41% Overweight on 13-Nov-26. Different broker pools; use FactSet for consistency with the revision data.

Anchor numbers (H1 FY2027, six months to 30-Sep-26, against H1 FY2026 restated). Sales CHF 1,834.1m (+6.7% CHF, +7.5% constant currency, about 7% organic; FX cost CHF 13.7m). Hearing Aid Wholesale CHF 968m (+11.0% cc, eleventh consecutive quarter of share gains); Retail CHF 749m (+6.5% cc: 5.2% organic, 1.3 points bolt-ons); Cochlear Implants CHF 117m (-10.2% local currency). Core gross margin 74.7% (72.4%); core EBIT CHF 384.0m (+21.4% CHF, +22.9% cc), margin 20.9% (18.4%); core EPS CHF 5.37 (3.96), +35.6%; reported EPS from continuing operations CHF 5.18. Operating free cash flow CHF 287.0m (89.7m); free cash flow CHF 266.2m (60.3m). Net debt CHF 1,072.4m, 1.1x. Inventory days 187 (163). ROCE 20.9%. Capex CHF 62.8m (46.2m).

Guidance FY2027 (raised 12-Nov-26): sales +6% to 8% in local currencies (from 5% to 8%); core EBIT +10% to 13% in constant currency (from 7% to 10%); market growth assumption 3% to 5% (from 2% to 4%); FX now broadly neutral for sales and core EBIT (previously a drag of 1 to 2 points on sales and 2 to 3 points on core EBIT). H2 contains a planned strategic investment step-up of a low double-digit CHF million amount. Mid-term (set March 2026): sales CAGR 5% to 10% local currency; core EBIT CAGR 7% to 12%; CHF 6bn revenue ambition by FY2030/31. Next results: FY2027 on 18 May 2027.

What the H1 numbers imply for H2 (the most important near-term modelling fact). With H1 core EBIT up 22.9% cc, the raised full-year range implies H2 core EBIT growth of only about 1% to 6% (DNB Carnegie's calculation; consistent with my base reconstruction), and the sales midpoint implies H2 wholesale growth of about 5% against 11% in H1 (Morgan Stanley). Morgan Stanley calls the raised guidance conservative and the upper end highly likely. My own working range for FY2027, for orientation only: sales about CHF 3,900m to 3,930m; core EBIT about CHF 860m to 880m (margin 22.0% to 22.5%); core EPS about CHF 12.0 to 12.3.
