Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 2

Research requests for the next set

Company: Sonova Holding AG (SOON-CH), the Swiss hearing-care group: Hearing Aid Wholesale (Phonak, Unitron, Hansaton and other brands sold to independent audiologists, the US Department of Veterans Affairs, Costco, retail chains and Sonova's own clinics), Hearing Aid Retail (about 3,600 owned clinics under AudioNova, GEERS, Boots Hearingcare and other banners) and Cochlear Implants (Advanced Bionics); Consumer Hearing (Sennheiser) is classified as discontinued and being sold.

Request: T-P1

Peers: Demant (closest listed peer; integrated wholesale plus retail, KIND acquisition in Germany, DKK 500 million cost programme); Amplifon (largest hearing-care retailer, now integrating GN Hearing; Fit4Growth cost programme; shows retail-stage cost structure); GN Store Nord (seller of GN Hearing; history of One-GN synergies and stranded costs); WS Audiology (private; second-largest hearing-aid maker, Signia and Widex); Starkey (private; US maker competing in independents); Cochlear Ltd (implant market leader, for the Advanced Bionics segment); Straumann (Swiss premium medtech with a franc cost base, for the currency-cost precedent). Inputs: What is the split of fixed and variable costs at the hearing-aid wholesale stage and at the retail clinic stage (audiologist pay, rent, marketing and lead generation as a share of clinic sales), and how does it differ between Sonova, Demant and Amplifon? How did Sonova's adjusted EBITA margin move in the volume downturns of FY2020/21, FY2023/24 (non-renewal of a large US contract) and the Mexicali manufacturing transfer in H1 FY2025/26, and in the upturns of FY2021/22, FY2025/26 and H1 FY2026/27 (company disclosures, 2020 to November 2026)? What did Sonova's past cost and footprint programmes promise and deliver, and over what timescale? What is known about the CHF 50 million transformation programme announced in March 2026 (CHF 90 million of annual savings by year four, net savings from year two, footprint, automation, value engineering): phasing, which cost lines, how much is reinvested, and how the Swiss-franc share of costs is to fall from about 15% to below 10%? What did Demant's DKK 500 million programme (announced February 2026) and Amplifon's Fit4Growth actually deliver against plan by late 2026? Which cost lines in hearing-aid manufacturing and retail have proved cuttable (manufacturing footprint, procurement, headquarters, IT) and which have not (R&D, audiologist labour, customer acquisition)? What is the size and nature of the "low double-digit million" strategic investment step-up Sonova flagged for H2 FY2026/27, and is it recurring? How large and recurring are Sonova's restructuring and transaction costs excluded from core EBIT (CHF 44 million in FY2024/25; CHF 35 million to 40 million guided for FY2026/27)? Focus: How much of Sonova's 250-basis-point H1 FY2026/27 core EBIT margin gain was operating leverage and lasting mix rather than the absence of the prior year's Mexicali ramp-up costs, and how much net margin the transformation programme can deliver by FY2029/30 after reinvestment and retail acquisition dilution.

Request: T-P2

Peers: Demant (Oticon Zeal and Reveal; the clearest current case of premium launch pricing in the US Veterans Affairs channel); WS Audiology (Signia; competes in independents and public channels); GN Hearing under Amplifon ownership (ReSound; pricing behaviour as it becomes captive); Starkey (private; reported as more promotional with independents); Amplifon (as a buyer: procurement prices per unit against independents); Cochlear Ltd (implant pricing under China volume-based procurement and US reimbursement); Straumann (premium out-of-pocket medtech pricing for comparison). Operating conditions: a crowded launch window with four premium platforms launched within weeks (August to September 2026); a soft private-pay consumer market with patients delaying upgrades (2023 to 2025 and channel checks in 2026); tariffs or import duties on hearing aids imported into the United States (the 2025 tariff episode and the pending Section 232 medical-equipment investigation); channel consolidation and buyer power (Costco, managed care, buying groups, Amplifon-GN, Demant-KIND); reimbursement pressure in public and insured channels (US Veterans Affairs contracts, US Medicare Advantage and Medicaid, German statutory insurance, French 100% Santé); a strong Swiss franc. Inputs: What price increases have hearing-aid makers achieved and held at wholesale list level since 2020, and when were prices given back through rebates or lower tiers? How did wholesale prices behave in the 2023 to 2025 market slowdown? What premium do Phonak Infinio Sphere and Phonak EON command over standard platforms in private and public channels, and how does that compare with Oticon Zeal's reported 42% premium and USD 695 Veterans Affairs price (Bernstein, 2026)? How are Veterans Affairs contract prices set and re-set (five-year indefinite-delivery contracts), and what were Sonova's and Demant's average selling prices in that channel in 2025 and 2026? What are wholesale prices by channel (independents, Veterans Affairs, Costco, managed care, large chains, national health services) and how is the channel mix shifting? How much of the 2025 tariff and input-cost increases did makers pass through, and with what lag (Amplifon reported price rises of over 10% with Phonak raising prices aggressively)? What do independent audiologists say in 2026 about Phonak's pricing, rebates and support compared with Oticon, Signia, ReSound and Starkey? How does Costco's contract structure work for suppliers, and what happened to Sonova's earlier Costco contract that was not renewed? How does retail average selling price behave in Sonova's own clinics, by country? Focus: Whether Sonova can hold or raise its wholesale price and mix through the crowded 2026 to 2027 launch window and growing buyer power, and how much a US tariff could be passed through and how quickly.

Request: T-P3

Why requested: Sonova's earnings path depends on two interacting loops, the two-year product cycle feeding share gains and premium mix, and vertical integration in retail changing which makers independent audiologists buy from, and their direction over 2027 to 2029 decides whether share gains persist or reverse. Peers: Demant (integrated retail plus wholesale; KIND conversion to its own products; Zeal's effect on its wholesale pull-through); Amplifon with GN Hearing (backward integration from retail into manufacturing); WS Audiology (competitor in independents; former employer of Sonova's chief executive and chief operations officer); GN Store Nord (historical share donor in the Veterans Affairs channel); Starkey (private competitor in US independents); Fielmann and EssilorLuxottica (optical chains entering hearing care). Inputs: Does a winning hearing-aid platform create a reinforcing loop (Veterans Affairs and independent clinician adoption, then retail pull-through, then higher R&D funding and the next win), and how long have such loops lasted historically for Phonak (Marvel 2018, Paradise 2020, Lumity 2022, Infinio 2024) and for Oticon (Intent, Zeal)? What stopped each loop? Does Sonova's monthly Veterans Affairs share lead its global wholesale share, and with what lag? Does owning retail clinics reinforce a maker's wholesale share, or provoke a balancing loop in which independent audiologists move purchases to non-integrated makers; what is the evidence from Demant's KIND integration (internal wholesale revenue +19% organically in Q1 2026, Rothschild & Co Redburn and Deutsche Bank) and from independents' reaction to Amplifon buying GN Hearing (expert calls, 2026)? Where does displaced GN Hearing independent and Costco volume go? Does rising customer acquisition cost in retail form a balancing loop on retail returns as clinics are acquired? Do the AI chip programmes raise the minimum efficient scale enough to keep pushing share toward the two largest makers? Focus: Whether the share gains Sonova has made since 2024 are self-reinforcing through FY2028/29 or are likely to mean-revert within one to two cycles, and whether vertical integration by Amplifon and Demant on balance takes volume from Sonova or sends independents toward it.

Request: T-P4

Why requested: Sonova's reported earnings per share have been driven as much by items below the operating line and by currency as by operations (normalised EPS in Swiss francs was broadly flat from FY2022/23 to FY2025/26 while local-currency earnings grew), so the bridge inputs need separate evidence. Peers: Demant (hedges cash flows up to 18 months; resumed buybacks October 2026); GN Store Nord (hedges 75% to 100% of expected EBITA on a 12-month rolling basis); Amplifon (euro reporter, does not hedge translation); Straumann and other Swiss medtech exporters with franc cost bases (Alcon, Ypsomed); Cochlear Ltd (implant segment comparison). Inputs: Swiss franc translation and transaction effects: how Sonova's sales and EBITA sensitivities to the US dollar and euro (company disclosure: 5% in USD/CHF moves sales by CHF 65 million and EBITDA by CHF 17 million; 5% in EUR/CHF moves sales by CHF 70 million and operating profit by CHF 25 million) have played out, and what published forecasters expect for USD/CHF and EUR/CHF to 2028. Effective tax rate: why it rose from single digits before FY2023/24 to about 16% to 18%, and the expected effect of the OECD global minimum tax and Swiss tax changes. Restructuring, transaction and litigation costs excluded from core EBIT, and product-liability provisions in cochlear implants. Share count: the paused CHF 1.5 billion buyback, management and broker statements on when buybacks may resume, and leverage policy (net debt to EBITDA corridor 1.0x to 1.5x). Retail acquisition spending (CHF 80 million to 100 million a year target) and its returns; what larger acquisitions the CHF 6 billion FY2030/31 revenue ambition requires. The Amplifon supply phase-out (CHF 50 million to 60 million of FY2025/26 revenue; DNB Carnegie) and its timing. Cochlear Implants recovery: Integro European rollout and US FDA approval timing, China volume-based procurement. Net interest cost and lease expenses. Focus: Which of currency, tax, buybacks and the Amplifon phase-out will move Sonova's earnings per share most between FY2026/27 and FY2029/30, and how far brokers' assumptions on each sit from management's statements and from the company's own history.

Layer 2: Translating the foundational components into an operating and financial model for the next 18 months, and 18 months to 3 years out

Written 24 November 2026, 07:51 to 08:07 UK, Opus, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a well-reasoned, coherent, deeply understood and accurate operating and financial model for the company for the next 18 months to four years, based on the first set's answers, a base of the company's historical financial and operating performance, and how businesses in the sector and industry outperform or underperform, so as to best help the Consumer achieve their objective, which is to produce a trustworthy judgement regarding the predictability of delivery of the financial forecasts over the next 18 months.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the likely effect on the company's financial forecasts is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Sonova (SOON-CH): Second set of questions, translating the foundations into an operating and financial model

Written 2026-11-24 by the Investment Analyst answering the second set, for the Investment Analyst answering the third and fourth sets. Fiscal years end 31 March (FY2027 = year to 31-Mar-27). All figures are continuing operations (Consumer Hearing is discontinued) in CHF millions unless stated.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Before the answers: the bases, the base case in one table, and where I depart from the first set

Bases used throughout (read these before comparing any figure with consensus).

  • Profit measure: Sonova's core EBIT. It includes amortisation of acquired intangibles (purchase price allocation, about CHF 30m a year by my reconstruction) and excludes restructuring, transaction and litigation costs and legacy cochlear product-liability charges (CHF 35m to 40m guided for FY2027). The FY2026 core EBIT base is not disclosed in the files; I reconstruct it at about CHF 780m (21.6% margin): H1 FY2026 restated core EBIT CHF 316.4m (disclosed) plus H2 about CHF 464m (H2 normalised EBITA about CHF 478m less about CHF 15m of acquired-intangible amortisation). The first set's range was CHF 775m to 790m; the Consumer should verify it from the 12-Nov-26 release.
  • EBITDA = core EBIT plus all depreciation and amortisation, including acquired-intangible amortisation and right-of-use (lease) depreciation under IFRS 16. On this basis FY2026 was about CHF 986m (27.3%), which equals FactSet's brokers'-basis actual, and H1 FY2027 about CHF 486m (26.5%; core EBIT 384.0 plus about 102 of depreciation and amortisation, my estimate from FactSet's H1 lines).
  • A deliberate departure from the forecast form. The form defines depreciation and amortisation as excluding acquired-intangible amortisation "as adjusted earnings do". Sonova's adjusted measure (core EBIT, core EPS) and the consensus built on it include that amortisation. I therefore put it inside my depreciation and amortisation input, so that the model's adjusted EPS equals Sonova's core EPS and is comparable with consensus. Excluding it would lift the model's EPS by about CHF 0.40 a share and create a false 3% gap to consensus.
  • EPS = core EPS (the company's measure, CHF 5.37 in H1 FY2027; FY2026 about CHF 10.34 on the brokers' basis). The FactSet consensus median for FY2027 (CHF 11.55) is stale; updated post-results brokers sit at CHF 11.56 to 12.60.
  • Currency. Guidance is in constant currency. My CHF forecasts carry an explicit franc assumption: FY2027 broadly neutral (H1 -0.8 points, H2 about +0.4 points at November spot, consistent with the company's "broadly neutral" full-year statement); FY2028 to FY2031 a drag of 1.0 point a year on sales and about 1.6 points a year on core EBIT. Consensus implicitly assumes zero.

My EPS is in line with updated brokers for FY2027 (and 4.5% above the stale median), in line with consensus for FY2028, and about 3% below for FY2029 and FY2030. The composition differs more than the total: my revenue is 2% to 6% lower (franc drag, a slower local-currency fade), my margin is 0.3 to 0.7 points higher, and my EPS includes buybacks that consensus does not. Remove the buybacks and my FY2030 EPS is about CHF 14.4 (-5% against consensus); remove the franc drag as well and it is about CHF 15.1, equal to consensus. Consensus for FY2029 and FY2030 is, in effect, my base case with no franc drag and no capital deployment.

Where I depart from, or add to, the first set (each is argued in the answers).

  1. Flat franc EPS from FY2023 to FY2026 was not only the franc. The reported tax rate rose from 8.1% (FY2023) to 16.0% (FY2026), which alone removed about 9% of EPS. That headwind is now largely complete; the franc headwind is not (P-08, P-09).
  2. The balance sheet falls below the 1.0x leverage floor a year earlier than the first set said: about 0.7x at March 2027 (not March 2028), and about 0.5x by March 2028 even with a modest buyback. The capital-allocation decision is therefore a May 2027 event, not a FY2028/29 event (P-02).
  3. At today's share price a retail acquisition is more EPS-accretive per franc than a buyback (an after-tax acquisition earnings yield of about 7% to 8% against a P/E earnings yield of about 4.7%), so management's stated preference for acquisitions is rational for EPS and negative for margin and ROCE (P-02).
  4. FY2027 sales: about 7.4% in local currency, the upper half of the 6% to 8% guidance rather than the top (the first set said about 8%); core EBIT +13%, the top of the 10% to 13% guidance (agreeing with the first set). I use an H2 core EBIT margin about 10 basis points below the prior year, not Morgan Stanley's 60 basis points (P-01, P-10).
  5. Franc drag from FY2028: I use 1.0 point on sales and 1.6 points on core EBIT, the lower part of the first set's 1 to 2 and 2 to 3 points, because the two published forecaster paths (Morgan Stanley, ING, November 2026) show a weaker euro but a firmer dollar against the franc in 2027 and recovery in 2028; the fifteen-year trend argues for more. The risk is skewed to a larger drag (P-06, P-09).
  6. Price is a margin defence, not a margin driver: about one point a year of price and mix roughly offsets cost inflation on a CHF 2.0bn operating-cost base; margin expansion must come from volume leverage and the savings programme (P-04).
  7. About two-thirds of H1 FY2027's 250-basis-point core EBIT margin gain is lasting (operating leverage, price and mix) and about one-third is the non-repeat of Mexicali ramp-up costs; H2 FY2027 has no such base effect (P-01, P-03).

Consensus segments (FactSet, FY2027 to FY2029): wholesale CHF 2,011m, 2,139m, 2,268m; retail CHF 1,603m, 1,726m, 1,849m; Cochlear Implants CHF 259m, 279m, 296m. I am above consensus on FY2027 wholesale (H2 still carries Costco and EON), below it on retail and Cochlear Implants (consensus implies an H2 FY2027 implant recovery of about 18% that the European-only Integro launch is unlikely to deliver), and below it on all segments from FY2028 because of the franc.

The operating drivers behind the inputs

  • Market: hearing-aid market value growth of about 5% in FY2027 (the August 2026 launch spike and the French renewal wave), about 4% in FY2028, about 3.75% thereafter (ageing and penetration; the cyclical premium annualises in H2 calendar 2027).
  • Wholesale: share gains of 3 to 4 points above market in FY2027 (Costco from March 2026, the VA, Virto R, EON), about 1.5 points in FY2028 (EON full year), 0.5 to 1.0 point after, as rivals catch up within one to two cycles; next platform assumed around August 2028. Amplifon's supply of CHF 50m to 60m (FY2026) phases out over FY2028 to FY2030 (roughly one-third a year), partly offset by independents moving away from GN. Price and mix about +1 to 1.5 points a year; VA prices fixed until about 2030.
  • Retail: organic growth of 4.5% to 5% (market plus positive ASP) plus 2 to 2.5 points from CHF 90m a year of bolt-ons; margin flat at mid-teens as customer acquisition costs rise.
  • Cochlear Implants: trough in H1 FY2027 (-10.2%); Integro in Europe from October 2026, US approval assumed in early calendar 2027; China volume-based procurement keeps that market at a low base. Margin recovers from about 6% toward 11% (high operating leverage).
  • Margin: core gross margin from 73.7% (FY2026) to about 75.1% (FY2027; Mexicali ramp-up costs gone, premium mix) and about 76% by FY2031 (footprint savings). Core EBIT margin +120 basis points in FY2027, then about +35 a year: volume leverage, net savings of about half the CHF 90m programme (CHF 10m, 25m, 40m, 45m net from FY2028 to FY2031), Cochlear Implants recovery; less the recurring H2 FY2027 investment step-up, retail mix and franc transaction effects.
  • Franc: FY2027 broadly neutral (as the company now guides); FY2028 to FY2031 a drag of 1.0 point a year on sales and about 1.6 points on core EBIT. Published paths (Morgan Stanley and ING, November 2026) show EUR/CHF near 0.90 in 2027 with the dollar firmer; the fifteen-year trend is a larger drag. This is the single largest swing factor in the forecasts.
  • Below EBIT: tax 17% rising to 18% (Pillar Two; the company expects 16.9% for FY2027); net financial costs CHF -14m in FY2027 (H1 helped by foreign-exchange gains) and CHF -22m to -25m after; non-core costs CHF 38m (FY2027) falling to CHF 20m.
  • Capital: capex rising to 3.6% to 3.7% of sales; dividend at about 43% of core EPS; leverage below the 1.0x to 1.5x corridor from March 2027; buybacks as the base-case proxy for deployment.

The company's guidance, and how your inputs compare with it

  • FY2027 guidance (raised 12-Nov-26): sales +6% to 8% in local currencies; core EBIT +10% to 13% in constant currency; FX broadly neutral for sales and core EBIT; market growth 3% to 5%; restructuring and transaction costs CHF 35m to 40m excluded from core; a low double-digit CHF million strategic investment step-up in H2. My inputs: sales +7.4% in local currency (+7.3% in francs), the upper half; core EBIT +13.2% (CHF 883m on a reconstructed base of about CHF 780m), the top; H2 core EBIT growth about +7.5% against the 1% to 6% the guidance range implies. I treat the guidance as conservative, as Morgan Stanley does, because H1 core EBIT grew 22.9% in constant currency and H2 still carries Costco and EON.
  • No FY2028 guidance yet (due 18-May-27). I expect a cautious first guide again; my FY2028 is local-currency sales +6.3% and core EBIT about +8.5% in constant currency (+6.9% in francs).
  • Mid-term framework (March 2026): sales CAGR 5% to 10% in local currency, core EBIT CAGR 7% to 12% in constant currency, CHF 6bn revenue ambition by FY2030/31, leverage 1.0x to 1.5x, dividend payout 40% to 45%, bolt-ons CHF 80m to 100m a year, buybacks suspended for FY2027. My inputs: local-currency sales CAGR FY2026 to FY2031 about 6.2% (lower half), core EBIT CAGR about 9% in constant currency (middle) and about 7.7% in francs. Revenue in FY2031 is CHF 4.7bn in francs and about CHF 4.9bn at constant currency: the CHF 6bn ambition needs about CHF 1.1bn of acquired revenue, which I do not model. Leverage falls below the corridor in my base case even with buybacks.

The bridge from the company's own reported figures to the basis consensus uses

  • Revenue: company continuing sales equal FactSet revenue for FY2026 (CHF 3,605.9m) and are the consensus basis. FactSet's estimates database shows FY2025 as first reported (CHF 3,865m, including Consumer Hearing), so do not compute growth from it. FactSet's half-year revenue lines (H2 FY2026 CHF 1,790m) do not reconcile to the company's halves (CHF 1,887.2m); use the company's.
  • Operating profit: company core EBIT (H1 FY2027 CHF 384.0m) = reported EBIT (CHF 372.0m) + non-core items (CHF 12.0m: restructuring CHF 6.7m, cochlear product-liability reassessment CHF 5.0m, other). Core EBIT includes amortisation of acquired intangibles; the old normalised EBITA (FY2026 CHF 811.2m, 22.5%) excluded it and is about CHF 30m higher a year. FactSet's history line "EBIT (adjusted)" (FY2026 CHF 743m, H1 FY2027 CHF 378m) is FactSet's own standardisation, neither core nor reported; consensus EBIT (CHF 859m for FY2027) is the brokers' figure, mostly on the core basis, with some stale estimates.
  • EBITDA: the brokers' basis (FY2026 CHF 986m, 27.3%) equals core EBIT plus all depreciation and amortisation including acquired intangibles and right-of-use assets; FactSet's operating EBITDA history (FY2026 CHF 968m, 26.9%) is after non-core items. My EBITDA input is on the brokers' basis.
  • EPS: core EPS (H1 FY2027 CHF 5.37; FY2026 about CHF 10.34 on the brokers' basis, CHF 10.42 on the old normalised basis) is what updated brokers forecast. Reported continuing EPS (H1 FY2027 CHF 5.18) is after non-core items; reported total EPS (FactSet basic FY2026 CHF 7.23) is also after the discontinued Consumer Hearing loss (H1 FY2027 CHF 56.8m after tax, about CHF 0.95 a share). The FactSet consensus median (FY2027 CHF 11.55) mixes stale pre-results estimates (RBC 10.16, Oddo 10.45, Vontobel 10.62, Barclays 10.74) with updated ones (CHF 11.56 to 12.60).
  • Free cash flow: FactSet's definition is operating cash flow less fixed-asset capex only (FY2026 CHF 340m); the company's operating free cash flow deducts all capex including intangibles but not lease principal (FY2026 CHF 519.1m), and its "free cash flow" also deducts acquisitions (CHF 473.7m); FactSet consensus free cash flow (FY2027 CHF 684m) is close to the company's operating free cash flow. My cross-check deducts capex, lease principal and restructuring cash and is before acquisitions, dividends and buybacks (FY2026 about CHF 434m); add about CHF 90m to 110m to compare with consensus.
  • Leverage: net debt CHF 1,072.4m at 30-Sep-26 (1.1x, company); FactSet CHF 1,078m (1.01x); both appear to include lease liabilities (CHF 240.9m at March 2026). One broker's 0.7x is on a different definition.
  • Dividend: FactSet dates dividends by payment period; my input is the dividend declared for each fiscal year (FY2026: CHF 4.70, paid June 2026).

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 3 and Layer 4

Research requests for the next set

Company: Sonova Holding AG (SOON-CH), the Swiss hearing-care group: Hearing Aid Wholesale (Phonak, Unitron, Hansaton and other brands sold to independent audiologists, the US Department of Veterans Affairs, Costco, retail chains and its own clinics), Hearing Aid Retail (about 3,600 owned clinics under AudioNova, GEERS, Boots Hearingcare and other banners) and Cochlear Implants (Advanced Bionics); Consumer Hearing (Sennheiser) is classified as discontinued and being sold; fiscal year ends 31 March, reports half-yearly.

Request: T-D1

Peers: Demant (closest listed peer: integrated wholesale and retail, Oticon Zeal and Reveal, KIND acquisition, DKK 500 million cost programme, buyback resumed October 2026); WS Audiology (private number-two maker, Signia and Widex; former employer of Sonova's chief executive and operations chief, Widex-Sivantos merger record 2019 to 2024); Amplifon with GN Hearing (largest retailer integrating backward into manufacturing; Fit4Growth programme); GN Store Nord (One-GN synergy delivery, seller of GN Hearing); Starkey (private US maker competing in independents); Cochlear Ltd (implant market leader, for Advanced Bionics); Straumann (Swiss premium medtech with a franc cost base, localisation of production and Pillar Two tax precedent); EssilorLuxottica (the chief executive's former employer; optical retail entering hearing care) Operating conditions: a crowded premium launch window (four platforms launched August to September 2026); a strong Swiss franc in a risk-off regime; the first two years of a new chief executive after a guidance reset; vertical integration of large retail chains (Amplifon-GN, Demant-KIND); soft private-pay consumer demand with upgrade deferrals; a pending US Section 232 medical-equipment investigation; capped reimbursement in Germany, France and US managed care; China volume-based procurement for cochlear implants Inputs: Has Sonova converted local-currency growth into growth in reported Swiss-franc earnings per share at any point since FY2019/20, and what took it away in each year (franc translation, the rise in the effective tax rate from single digits to about 16% to 18%, perimeter changes, one-off costs)? How long have wholesale share gains from a winning hearing-aid platform lasted historically (Phonak Marvel 2018, Paradise 2020, Lumity 2022, Infinio 2024; Oticon Opn, More, Intent, Zeal; ReSound One) and what ended each run? What did Sonova's previous cost programmes promise and deliver (including the CHF 40 million run-rate savings reported in May 2025), and how much of the savings reached operating margin after reinvestment; how does that compare with Demant's DKK 500 million programme, GN's One-GN and Amplifon's Fit4Growth? What is the record of the current chief executive and operations chief on cost, integration and margin at WS Audiology and Essilor? Have hearing-care makers rolled up retail clinics at CHF 80 million to 100 million a year without diluting return on invested capital (Sonova AudioNova 2016, Demant Audika 2015 and KIND 2025, Amplifon 2015 to 2025)? Has Advanced Bionics ever recovered margin and share after a processor launch or recall (2006, 2020), and how quickly? How fast have Swiss exporters reduced the franc share of their costs (Straumann Shanghai and Mansfield; Sonova's target of below 10% from about 15%)? Has any hearing-aid maker delivered core margin expansion of the size implied by Sonova's March 2026 margin bridge (+2 to 3 points from footprint, +1 to 2 from operating leverage, minus 1 to 3 points of strategic investment)? Focus: Whether the earnings growth now expected for Sonova from FY2026/27 to FY2029/30 rests on strengths the company has demonstrably shown (such as wholesale platform wins, the lowest manufacturing cost, strong cash generation) or on strengths still to be demonstrated under the new team (such as net savings after reinvestment, returns on retail acquisitions, an implant recovery, a lower franc cost share, and growth in reported francs rather than local currency).

Request: T-D2

Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 25 November 2026, 04:50 to 05:15 UK, Fable, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Sonova (SOON-CH): Third and fourth sets of questions

Written 2026-11-25 by the Investment Analyst answering the third and fourth sets, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo. Fiscal years end 31 March (FY2027 = year to 31-Mar-27). Continuing operations, CHF millions, unless stated. "Core EBIT" and "core EPS" are the company's measures: after amortisation of acquired intangibles, before restructuring, transaction and litigation costs.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Before the answers: one correction to the base, the model gap explained, and the base case as it now stands

1. The FY2026 core EBIT base is CHF 762.9m (21.2% margin), not about CHF 780m. This changes the reading of FY2027. The first set reconstructed the base at CHF 775m to 790m and the second set at about CHF 780m, both assuming about CHF 30m a year of acquired-intangible amortisation. The FY2025/26 annual report (AlphaSense report on the restated base) gives core EBIT of CHF 762.9m, which is normalised EBITA of CHF 811.2m less CHF 48.3m of acquisition-related amortisation. Two independent checks agree: FactSet's EBITDA on the brokers' basis (CHF 986m) less FactSet's total depreciation and amortisation (CHF 226m) is CHF 760m, and FactSet's two half-year adjusted EBIT lines (CHF 316m and CHF 445m) sum to CHF 761m. Consequences:

  • The second half of FY2026 earned core EBIT of CHF 446.5m on CHF 1,887m of sales, a margin of 23.7%, not the 24.6% the second set used.
  • Guidance of +10% to 13% is CHF 839m to 862m. The second set's FY2027 core EBIT of CHF 883m, which it described as "+13.2%, the top of guidance", is in fact +15.7%, almost three points above the range. That was not its intended judgement.
  • The last twelve months to September 2026 already show core EBIT of CHF 830m on sales of CHF 3,721m, a 22.3% margin.
  • My FY2027 base is core EBIT of about CHF 864m (+13%, margin 22.3%): the second half at about CHF 480m, a margin of about 23.6%, slightly below the prior second half's 23.7%. This sits between Morgan Stanley (second-half margin about 60 basis points lower, about CHF 854m) and an unchanged margin (CHF 866m); it equals the top of guidance, Bernstein's 22.2% margin, DNB Carnegie's CHF 866m and FactSet consensus (CHF 859m to 868m). I carry the same 50-basis-point reduction through every later year, because the second set's yearly margin gains (about 35 to 40 basis points) did not depend on the base.

2. The gap the model summary flags between the second set's cross-check and the model, explained.

  • Earnings per share (model 2.0% to 3.2% below the cross-check in every year). One cause explains it: the model builds FY2027 revenue as the reported first half plus the second half, and it applies the second-half growth input (7.8%) to FactSet's figure for the second half of FY2026, about CHF 1,791m, when the company's continuing-operations figure is CHF 1,887m (FactSet's half-year line appears to have been struck before the first half was restated for the Consumer Hearing exit). The model's FY2027 revenue is therefore CHF 3,765m (+4.4%) instead of CHF 3,869m (+7.3%), and every later year carries the same 2.7% shortfall, worth about 3.4% of EPS. Two smaller items work the other way: the model does not deduct minorities (CHF 3m to 4m, about CHF 0.06 a share) and it holds a slightly lower share count from FY2029. The second set warned of the base problem in its inputs; the model did not act on it. The input as written is wrong for the model's base, so I change it (to 13.6% on the model's base, which reproduces second-half revenue of CHF 2,035m; see inputs-changed.md).
  • Free cash flow (model CHF 28m above the cross-check in FY2027, equal in FY2028, CHF 6m to 14m below after). Two opposite effects: the revenue shortfall lowers the model's EBITDA by CHF 29m to 37m a year, while the model does not deduct the cash cost of restructuring and other non-core items (CHF 38m in FY2027 falling to CHF 20m), which the cross-check does; in FY2027 the model's working-capital build is also CHF 21m smaller on its lower revenue. No cash-flow input is wrong. The memo Analyst should read the model's equity free cash flow as stated before non-core cash costs of CHF 20m to 38m a year.
  • A third distortion, not flagged, that matters for the memo: dividend timing. The model pays each fiscal year's dividend input inside that fiscal year, while the company pays it the following June. FY2027 therefore carries two dividends (the CHF 279.8m paid in June 2026 and about CHF 310m for FY2027): this is the only reading consistent with the model's FY2027 cash flow of CHF -32m and its year-end net debt of CHF 1,076m. Two results follow. (a) The model's net debt is about one dividend too high at every March year-end, so it shows leverage of 1.0x at March 2027 when the company's own measure will be about 0.75x (my estimate: net debt of about CHF 0.8bn including leases; DNB Carnegie 0.8x). The second set's 0.67x appears too low: it treats lease repayments as reducing net debt without allowing for new leases, and DNB Carnegie's 0.8x supports the higher figure. (b) The 18-month return counts two dividends (+3.7%) when a buyer today receives one before 31-Mar-28 (+1.8%); the three-year column is right. The dividend input itself is correct, so I do not change it.
  • The Financial Analyst should correct the second-half FY2026 revenue base to CHF 1,887.2m (and then use 7.8%, not 13.6%, for the half-year to 31-Mar-27: one or the other, not both); should pay each year's dividend in the first half of the following fiscal year; and should deduct non-core cash costs of CHF 38m, 30m, 25m, 20m and 20m from equity free cash flow.

3. The base case as it now stands (revenue unchanged from the second set; only the margin and the model's base are changed).

What the correction does to the comparison with consensus. The second set said its margin was above consensus and its EPS in line for FY2027 and FY2028. With the right base, my margin equals consensus in every year (22.3%, 22.7%, 23.0%, 23.4% against 22.3%, 22.7%, 23.1%, 23.7%), FY2027 is in line on every line, and I am about 2.5% below consensus on revenue, EBIT and EPS in FY2028 and about 5% to 7% below on EBIT and on EPS in FY2029 and FY2030. The revenue gap by FY2030 (5.4%) is three points of franc and 2.4 points of local-currency growth (consensus grows 7.3%, 6.4%, 6.4% from FY2028; I use 6.3%, 5.6%, 5.8%). In FY2028 the shortfall against consensus by segment is CHF 14m in wholesale, CHF 37m in retail and CHF 19m in Cochlear Implants: the disagreement is not about the launch window.

Co-movement of SOON-CH with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-11-24 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Question: For Sonova Holding AG (SOON-CH), which EUR/CHF, USD/CHF and other exchange rates does each covering broker state or imply in its forecasts for FY2027/28, FY2028/29 and FY2029/30 published after the half-year results of 12 November 2026 (name each house: Morgan Stanley, Bernstein, Jefferies, DNB Carnegie, Deutsche Bank, UBS, Goldman Sachs, Barclays, RBC, Oddo BHF, Vontobel, Zurcher Kantonalbank and any others), and how many of them model a currency effect other than zero on sales and on core EBIT in those years; what do market forward rates for EUR/CHF, USD/CHF and GBP/CHF imply for the average rate in each of those fiscal years against the average of FY2026/27; what first guidance for FY2027/28 do brokers expect Sonova to give on 18 May 2027, and with what currency and market-growth assumptions; and how did Sonova's share price and consensus earnings estimates move on and after each past occasion when its first full-year guidance was set below consensus or stated a currency headwind (May 2023, May 2024, May 2025, the strategy update of March 2026, and May 2026, when guidance stated a drag of 1 to 2 points on sales and 2 to 3 points on core EBIT)?

Request: OWN-2

Question: Who owns and who is short Sonova Holding AG (SOON-CH) after the rise in its share price during 2026, and what are investors, as distinct from brokers, saying about it: how have the largest institutional holdings, the number and type of funds holding the shares, short interest as a share of the free float (Bernstein reported above 19% in late 2025) and stock-lending data changed since May 2026; what do fund-manager letters, investor interviews, questions asked on earnings calls and at conferences, brokers' positioning and sales-desk notes, and investor commentary on X (Twitter), Substack, Seeking Alpha and similar forums say about Sonova since the results of 12 November 2026; which arguments recur among holders and among sceptics, and which subjects appear rarely or not at all (the Swiss franc beyond FY2026/27, the tax rate, the use of the balance sheet, the comparison base in the first half of FY2027/28, Amplifon, US tariffs, cochlear implants); and what does the same evidence show for Demant, Amplifon and GN Store Nord?

Request: OWN-3

Question: How have the share prices, the forward price-to-earnings multiples and the consensus earnings estimates of hearing-aid makers behaved in the 6 to 18 months after wholesale growth peaked in a product cycle and the comparison base became harder: for Sonova Holding AG (SOON-CH) after Phonak Marvel (2019 to 2020), after Paradise and the post-pandemic reopening (2021 to 2022, including the guidance cut of August 2022), after Lumity (2023) and after Infinio (2025); for Demant after Oticon Opn (2017 to 2018), More (2021 to 2022) and Intent (2024 to 2025); and for GN Store Nord after ReSound One? For each episode give the date and level of the peak in organic wholesale growth, the forward multiple at that peak and 6, 12 and 18 months later, the change in consensus earnings over the same periods, the share price reaction on the results days when the slowdown was first reported, and what distinguished the episodes in which the multiple held (for example a buyback, a further rise in guidance or an acquisition) from those in which it fell; and at what forward multiples did Sonova trade during its periods of strongest delivery, compared with its ten-year median?
